# PROGRESS

**The one file that answers "where am I".** House rule 1: one piece of state has
exactly one owner. This is the owner of *current position*. Nothing else may
restate it — other files reference a gate id, they never say which gate is active.

Read this first, every session. Update it before you close, in the same commit as
the work (house rule 6).

---

原本的方法量不到自己的頭號預測** —— `CONFIG_GENERIC_TIME=y` 且選中的是 `clocksource_jiffies`,`getnstimeofday()` 只在 `jiffies` 變時才變,所以核心報的時間走 **10 ms 的格子**:60 s 上是 **166.7 ppm**,而訊號是 **17.99 ppm**,**差九倍**;§5.3 的三支不是一格的三種結果,它們需要三種不同的基線(`CLK-20` 就地補述)。🟢 **兩個都有現成的解,而且驅動本來就在印那個解**:引用 `tc1_cycles` 並在核心外用 `jiffies` 還原圈數 —— **安全係數 469.8 倍,而那是兩條路徑裡比較差的那一條**:半個週期是 67,108,863 counts = **4,697.5 ms**,一個 jiffy 是 10 ms,`4697.5 / 10 = 469.8`;卡片實際用的 `ΔTC0_total` 沒有 10 ms 格子,即使 TC1 與 TC0 差到 100 ppm,30 s 上仍有 **1,566 倍**、60 s 上 **783 倍**。**引用低的那個是刻意的** —— 它在 `Q3` 被否證(`tc0cnt` 不是 bits 31:4)的情況下仍然成立;用 `TC0_total = jiffies × 142858 + (tc0cnt >> 4)` 當參考,把 100 Hz 的格子換成連續的 14.29 MHz 計數器,**一個 count 在 60 s 上是 0.0012 ppm**,而 `+17.99 ppm` 從此是兩個常數的算術結果而不是要拿碼錶追的量。⚠️ **`>> 4` 不是假設,它早就量過了** —— `REG-05` 的 `TC0DATA = 0x0022E0A0 = 142,858 << 4`、`REG-07` 的 `TC0CNT = 0x0010B960`(bits 3:0 為零),而 `probe3` 的 Group T 已經在矽片上用過這個移位。卡片的 `Q3` 加的是**新狀態下的交叉檢查**(第一次在 Linux 下、在 shell 裡讀它)而不是第一次測試,**而這一句的初稿把它寫成了後者**。🟢 **`E8` 的「≈0 ppm」那一支在桌面上被排除了 —— 而 §5.3 說它是這一格最值錢的副產物,所以那是花掉的價值而不是賺到的,這一列不把兩件事寫成一件。** 量,在我自己建的 `vmlinux`(sha256-16 `2b0d1618d9946cc6`)上,沒有執行任何 vendor binary、沒有打開任何 vendor 原始碼:`clocksource_register` 在整個 `PT_LOAD` 只有 **2** 個直接轉移(`init_jiffies_clocksource+0x4` 的尾呼叫 `j`、`rtl819x_tc_write_proc+0x27c` 的 `jal`)、它的位址被具體化 **0** 次(所以沒有東西能 `jalr` 到它)、`# CONFIG_MODULES is not set` —— **廠商一個 clocksource 都沒註冊**(`CLK-21`)。🔴 **正控制第一次沒發射,而那正是它抓到的東西**:v1 只數 `jal`、回報 1,設計好的正控制 `init_jiffies_clocksource` 沒響而 `panic`(45)／`schedule`(72) 有 —— 因為 `return clocksource_register(...)` 是**尾呼叫**,gcc 發 `j` 不是 `jal`;間接掃描的前三個正控制(`panic`／`do_timer`／`clocksource_get_next`)也全是 0,因為**它們的位址根本沒被取過**,換成 `rtl819x_tc_read_proc`(2)／`rtl819x_tc_write_proc`(1)／`rtl819x_tc1_clocksource`(3) 才是會發射的。這是一次對廠商**編譯後**程式碼的讀取,記進 `docs/blind-write-ledger.md` **§4.3.1**,新的第四種深度 `artefact`;🟢 **限制傷害的是順序而不是論證** —— 驅動在這次掃描之前就已經編譯、連結、釘進映像,而映像的 sha256 是證人。🆕 **carried forward 那條「樣板還在錯」的殘留變成檢查器了。** `cardcheck` **27 → 31 個控制**:`FLR` 走 `--send` 就是繞過 `flrbracket run`(唯一會在開埠之前拒絕把前讀寫進這個 repo 的東西),`A19` 抓它、`A20` 放行兩張**具名的**凍結卡、`A21` 是「這是守衛不是毯子」的控制(另外五個 loader verb 一個都不能被碰)、`B10` **雙向**掃 50 張卡(2 張用 `FLR`,清單剛好 —— 反方向擋的是清單靜靜長成毯子)。變異 **18 → 22,22／22 全殺**;🔴 **而 `M6` 的 anchor 被我這次編輯打斷時,是它自己 FAIL 而不是靜靜通過**,那是變異器該有的行為。🔴 **帳本自己被自己的掃描抓出兩處不精確**:`kernel/time/jiffies.c` 宣告深度 `name` 而 `scan` 報 `line`(`notes/timer-driver.md:273` 引了 `:37`),**而 `ledgerscan check` 比對的是路徑集合、從不看深度欄**,所以少宣告深度可以一路綠(§7 ⑧);`rlx-time.c` 那一列的「zero citations」現在數字上是假的(6 次引用、4 個檔),**而主張本來就該是「什麼都沒拿」,引用數從來不是證據**。⚠️ **`config/` 一個字都沒動** —— 量:15 個檔,`RECIPE_ID` 用 `rlxfw-kbuild.sh:203` 的同一條指令重新導出仍是 **`229d2983`**,所以 `rlxfw-r51-20260903.bin`(**1,030,144** bytes,sha256-16 `39abf11c2d6fd0ce`)沒有失效,板子必須印 `RLXFW-ID0=229D2983` **收工的三個量測。** ① **CI run `33747027566` 四個 job 全綠**(`lint`／`text` 48 步／`instruments` 16 步／`census`),run wall **577 s**。🔴 **`CI-5` 拿到第四與第五個資料點,而第五個抓到我在這一列寫錯的一句話。**第一版寫「`instruments` 的 exec 562 s,對上 560／561／1183,suite 那一側四次落在 ±0.4 % 內」—— **錯,因為 `exec` 是所有 step 的和而 `apt` 在裡面**,1183 正是被那次 650 s 的 `apt` 撐起來的,它不可能同時是「suite 側穩定」的證據。**承載那個宣稱的量是 `exec − apt`**。量,五次:**536／537／533／533／523 s**(對應 `apt` 24／24／650／29／35),全距 14 s ≈ **±1.3 %**,而不是 ±0.4 %。🟢 **結論本身沒有倒,只是變小了**:wall 的 26 倍變異仍然全在 `apt`,suite 側五次三天落在 2.6 % 的帶內。🆕 **而今天兩次相隔 29 分鐘的 run 逐 step 拆開,母體幾乎相同**(只差兩個散文檔):每一支 suite 都在 ±5 s 內,最大的兩個是`test-spec-check-mutants` −5 s 與 `test-console-capture-mutants` −3 s,**而 `apt` 自己 +6 s** —— 逐 step 的證據比逐 job 的更強,因為它顯示沒有任何一支 suite 在動。🟢 **CI 的 `census` 是仲裁者,而它從執行結果重新導出我今天改的兩個宣告數**:`cardcheck ran 31/31`、`test-cardcheck-mutants ran 22/22`。② **本機 sweep,對一棵凍結的樹**:用 PyYAML 解 `ci.yml`、把每個 `run:` 的值逐字交給 `bash -c`,**58 步、1,441.9 s、56 綠、2 紅**。🟢 **58 = CI 實際的 2＋43＋11＋2,逐字相符,而且含 `verify-backup-copy`** —— 那正是上一段被 regex 掃描靜靜漏掉的那一支。兩條紅都在 `census` job 而且都是它在工作:`census.03` 印`RED test-hazlint`／`RED test-hazlint-objs`(`*bench-only*` 卻有真的 `.out`)加 `NOT-RUN-TOTAL MISMATCH: declares 478 and this job did not run 2`;🔴 **`census.02` 在本機必然失敗**,它 `cp dl/*/*.out` 而 `dl/` 是 GitHub 下載 artifact 的目錄 —— **上一段的記述沒有點名這一條,因為那次數的是 suite 而這次數的是 step,兩個母體不同**。⚠️ **第一次 sweep 作廢了,而作廢它的是我自己**:它跑到第 22 步時我還在改檔案,而第 2 步 `spec-check` 掃的是全部 94 個 `.md` —— **對一棵會動的樹做 sweep 量不到任何連貫的東西**,砍掉重跑。③ **`flashwin scan`,唯一能問「已提交的檔案裡有沒有禁止內容」的工具,而它結構上只能在這台機器跑**(探針集從參考 dump 切出來,而 dump 不能提交):🟢 **rlxfw 自己的樹 CLEAN,1,602 個檔案**、113 個 16-byte 探針;預設 sweep 1,904 檔仍然只有那一個既有 ### The four things this file tracks, because they are not the same thing

This line existed to name one of them and was read as naming all four. **Active
step above is a bench session, which is not one of `DAY-ZERO` items 0–8** — that
is not an error, but nothing said so.

| | what it is | where it lives | answers |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Gates** `S0`, `R0`…`R9`, `P1`…`P4` | the fifteen milestones | the board below | *how far is the project* |
| **`DAY-ZERO` items 0–8** | 🔄 **the instruments that had to exist before *any* gate could run** — not "the desk step list for the gate that is active now", which is what this row said until 2026-08-25 and which made it a **second owner** of the state § *Step list* owns. `item 7` had already drawn the line the row missed: `hazlint` is `DAY-ZERO`'s, the hazard verdicts it enables are `R1b`'s | `plan/DAY-ZERO.md` | *what had to exist before the first gate* |
| **§ Step list** 🆕 | the session-sized steps of the gate that is active now, written on entering it (`plan/SESSIONS.md` §0b) | **this file**, above | *what do I do next at the desk* |
| **Runsheet sessions** `B1`, `B2`, `B3` | what gets typed at the bench. 🔄 **Not "one power cycle each", which is what this row said until 2026-08-24**: `B1` and `B2` share one; `B3` costs **two of its own**, because `G6`'s and `G7`'s jumps each end with a kernel running and the loader gone; and a session outlives a seating — `B1` has now been typed across three power cycles, one directory each | `RUNSHEET.md` | *what do I do next at the device* |
| **`C-n`** | open questions that outlive one session, each owned by a gate | Carried forward, below | *what is still unanswered* |

**Runsheet sessions are not plan items and never were.** They exist because
`DAY-ZERO` items 2c and 4 produced a large number of claims read out of a flash
dump, a datasheet and four vendors' source, and **no number in this repository
has been measured on the device**. A session appears when enough desk claims
have piled up to be worth one power cycle. `B1` and `B2` are the first, and
`B3` is the one that closes the gate that is active.

A `C-n` is *not* extra work appearing from nowhere either: it is the mechanism
for **not** doing work now. When a session turns up a question it cannot close,
the choice is to chase it (and lose the thread) or to write it down with the
gate that owns it. The list is long because the desk work has been productive,
and every row names where it gets settled.

---

## Step list for the active gate — `R5`

**Opened 2026-09-02** on the owner's decision, in the same segment `R4` closed.
**Est. 24 段** (the plan's; the gate board's column says 24 too, so for once the
two numbers agree and there is nothing to reconcile). ⚠️ **Uncalibrated like
every other row** — the two closed gates with comparable definitions ran at
**0.5–0.57×** the plan, and applying that here would say ~13. That ratio has
n = 2 and is not applied; it is written down so a surprise is visible.

### 🔴 Three things this gate has to settle before a driver is written

**① The blind-write premise is partly spent, and how much is a question with an
answer rather than a worry.** `docs/driver-diff.md` is the deliverable the plan
calls rarer than the six drivers, and its whole value rests on *two independent
implementations*: **write blind, then diff register by register.** 量 today,
by asking what this repository has already read rather than by assuming:

* **`ggbruno/openwrt` is NOT cloned.** `SOURCES.json` carries it with
  `"fetch": "later"` and `"needed_by": "R10b"`, and `src-vendor/` holds four
  vendor drops and no third-party port. What has been read is **the author's
  forum claim** that gpio/pci/irq/timers work — and `SOURCES.json`'s own caveat
  already says *"'Working' is a forum claim, not a measurement"*.
* **`shibajee/linux-rtl8196e`'s `rtl8196e.dtsi` HAS been read**, and twice used:
  `CPU-25`'s advance cache-geometry prediction and `notes/cache-model.md` §386.
  🟢 **But the thing that would have spoiled the diff is not in it**: this
  repository already recorded that *that file's register addresses are
  placeholders and `dtc` refuses to parse it*. So the **register maps** — which
  is what `driver-diff` compares — were not seen.

🔴 **`R5-0` freezes this in writing before the first driver, because after that
"I had not seen it" is unverifiable.** Per driver, one line: what was read, when,
and what was taken from it. **A blind-write claim with no ledger is not a claim.**

**② `--keep` and the incremental build, and this is the gate that has to pay for
them or drop them.** `R4-0` measured `--keep` at 3–5 % and it refuses to run with
`--marks` at all; `notes/incremental-build.md` closed the 599-object rebuild and
then measured that the fix **buys zero on the loop's default path**, because
every build re-stages a fresh tree with no objects to keep. `INC-1` is the hole:
**the incremental cost of one REAL edit on a reused tree has never been
measured** — every number so far is a no-op or a `touch`, and touching a file is
not editing it. **R5 is ~24 segments of exactly that edit.** So this is where the
measurement pays for itself or is dropped on the record.

**③ Which driver is first is not a free choice, and the answer is forced.**
`CPU-42` makes the SoC timer a prerequisite, and the plan's own bring-up order —
clock → interrupt → timer → everything else — puts it first anyway.
🔴 **And irqchip is deliberately NOT in this gate**, for three stated reasons
(2.6.30 has no `irq_domain`, so the shape written here would not be the one a
modern BSP uses; it is the most deeply coupled to vendor platform code and
admits no coexistence experiment; and `R6` has to touch interrupts regardless).
**`R5` ships `docs/interrupt-map.md` instead**, which `R6` needs anyway.

| Step | | What it produces | DoD | Where it is most likely to be wrong |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **`R5-0`** ✅ | desk 1 | **The blind-write ledger, the loop's seam, and the driver order.** ① what this repository has already read of each public port, per driver, dated; ② whether `R5`'s first bench iteration runs **without** `--skip S2,S3`, which closes `SEAM-1` for free; ③ `INC-1`'s experiment designed, since `R5` is the gate that needs it | The ledger is committed **before** any driver source exists, so its ordering is checkable by `git log` the way `notes/dev-loop.md` §1–§3 are. `SEAM-1` and `INC-1` each get a decision, not a deferral | **That the ledger reads as an excuse rather than a boundary.** If it ends up listing everything as "seen", `driver-diff` is not worth writing and saying so is the correct outcome |
| **`R5-1`** ✅ | desk 2 | **`rtl819x-timer`, clocksource only, written blind**, registered at a LOW rating so it **coexists** with the vendor's. ~300 lines | It compiles, `hazlint` is clean over it, and the two-clocksource comparison is designed with its refutation written first. ✅ **2026-09-03, all three**: 596 `CC` and 0 diagnostics on this file; `hazlint` 0 violations in 100 loads with `K2`/`K4`/`K4b` firing; `notes/timer-driver.md` § 5 carries nine cells with their refutations. ⚠️ **The comparison changed shape and the change is recorded rather than absorbed**: *two clocksources compared against each other* is **circular** over any interval longer than a tick, because both derive from one crystal and the ratio reduces to `14,286,057 / (100 × 142,858)`. It is a **three-way** comparison now — mine, the kernel's time base, and the host's wall clock — and the first pair is worth taking because it measures **lost ticks**, not the oscillator | **The divisor.** `CDBR` is 14 by 讀; if the reading is wrong the two clocksources diverge, and that is a finding about `CLK-06` rather than about the driver. 🔄 **2026-09-03: half right.** The divisor is still the driver's only external dependency, but `CLK-06` moved 推 → 讀 the same day (D Table 26 states it), so a divergence now points at **`CLK-19`** — whether a timer's period is `N` or `N+1` counts, worth 7 ppm and inside `CLK-02`'s own error bar — rather than at the divisor |
| **`R5-2`** ✅ | bench 1 | **DONE 2026-09-03, seating 11, one power cycle.** 🟢 `Q6` held at **0.000 ppm over three intervals, residual exactly zero**, the longest 703.46 s / 140,693,532 counts across one `2^27` wrap. 🔴 **Two of the DoD's own terms turned out not to mean what they say, and both were found on the device**: the comparison was **not** against `/proc/timer_list` (the driver's own `/proc` file carries both counters in one `spin_lock_irqsave`, which `timer_list` cannot), and it is **not** a frequency against `CLK-02` — `wall`, `jiffies` and TC1 all descend from one divider, so what is 量 is the **ratio** `TC1 : tick = 2000 : 1` and the absolute 200.005 kHz stays 推. The only independent time base available, the host `.timing`, disagreed with itself by 17× its own floor. **The `± 50 ppm` bar is met by three orders of magnitude and the `sleep 60` bar is not measurable the way it is written** — `notes/timer-driver.md` § 8.2, `bench/2026-09-03/CORRECTIONS-block8.md` § 4. ~~The two-clocksource reading on the silicon~~ — `/proc/timer_list` showing both, compared against each other and against `CLK-02`'s 200.0049 MHz ± 7 ppm | Frequency within **± 50 ppm** of `CLK-02`, and `sleep 60` wall-clock error under 0.1 %. 🔴 **Zero risk by construction**: at a low rating nothing switches to it, so a wrong driver is a wrong number and not a board that will not boot | **That the coexistence is not real** — if 2.6.30's clocksource core picks one and stops reading the other, the comparison is one number and not two, and the step has to become something else |
| **`R5-3`** | desk + bench | **clockevent, and the rating raised** — the first change that can stop the board booting | The board boots with my timer as the system time base, ten times, no oops. **The rescue path is `R4`'s scripted reset**, and it is why this order is affordable at all | 🔴 **This is the first irreversible-feeling step of the gate.** A wedged board costs a power cycle, not the unit — but the ten boots are what makes it a result rather than an anecdote |
| **`R5-4`**…**`R5-8`** | — | **The other five drivers**, in the plan's order: `rtl819x-gpio`, `rtl819x-spi`+MTD, `rtl819x-wdt`, `leds-rtl819x`, `gpio-keys`. Each decomposes into desk/bench when it is reached; they are not enumerated here because the first one will teach what the split costs | Each: accepted upstream-style, **10 boots without an oops**, plus a `.dts` node and a `bindings/*.yaml` that `dtc` and `dt-validate` accept, **marked *not probed on hardware*** | **The MTD one.** Its acceptance is `mtd_debug read` of 4 MiB matching `FLS-14`'s sha256, and it has to handle the D-cache holding stale data from the `0xBD000000` window after a write |
| **`R5-9`** | desk | **`docs/driver-diff.md`** — blind first, then **two layers scored separately** (`D3`) against both public trees. 🔴 **And the FIRST operation on each cloned tree is a derivation check** — headers, copyright lines, SPDX tags, symbol names against `arch/rlx`'s — **before any register map is read**, with its result written down before proceeding | Every L2 row has a verdict or an explicit 未定 with the experiment that would settle it; every L1 row is a cross-check with a source. **The ledger from `R5-0` is quoted in it**, because a diff's value is exactly the independence it can demonstrate | 🔴 **That the third-party ports DERIVE from the vendor's `arch/rlx`** — `R5-0` § 4.5 records deep reading of the vendor's MTD map, and if `shibajee`'s peripheral drivers are ports of Realtek's then that reading is reading *their ancestor*. It cannot be settled before cloning, and cloning is the act the ledger dates — which is why the derivation check is an **ordering constraint** imposed now rather than a question asked later |
| **`R5-10`** | desk | **`docs/interrupt-map.md`** — `GIMR`/`GISR`/`IRR1`–`IRR3` routing, the four arming layers a timer interrupt needs, and the values already measured | It is written as `R6`'s prerequisite, not as a consolation for the missing irqchip | — |
| **`R5-11`** | desk 1 | **The write-up**, and `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md` gains its **eighth** entry | The DoD read one row at a time, what the gate did not establish written, the operating clause re-run over eight entries | — |

### The DoD, split into what can be refuted

* **D1** six drivers in-tree, each accepted, **10 boots without an oops** each.
* **D2** each has a compile-tested DT binding, **marked *not probed on hardware***.
* **D3** 🔄 **restated 2026-09-02 at `R5-0`, into two layers scored
  separately**, because the original surface could not measure what it was
  chosen to measure. `docs/driver-diff.md` exists, written blind first, and
  each row is scored on the layer it belongs to:
  **L1 fact** — register addresses, reset values, documented bit positions.
  Three implementations describing *one piece of silicon*; agreement is
  expected and says nothing about independence, so this layer is a
  **cross-check**, not a diff.
  **L2 decision** — divisor semantics (`CLK-06`, 推), wrap handling for a
  modulus that is not a power of two, interrupt number, which bits are
  write-1-to-clear, initialisation order, `clocksource` `mult`/`shift`,
  rating and coexistence. **This is the layer the ledger protects**, and every
  L2 row carries a verdict or an explicit 未定 with the experiment that would
  settle it.
  🔴 **The objection to this change is recorded with it**: `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md`'s
  operating clause warns that changing a rule because it failed to produce the
  answer you had already reached is how such a rule stops being an instrument.
  The distinction claimed — no conclusion had been reached; what was found is
  that **L1 agreement is guaranteed by the parts being the same part**, which
  is instrument validity — and **the test that settles it: the new `D3` is
  EASIER to fail.** *Register by register* passes almost by construction;
  the two-layer form lets `R5-0`'s ledger **void** rows outright (§ 4.5).
  A change that makes a DoD more refutable is not a target being moved.
* **D4** the timer is the system time base and `/proc/timer_list` agrees with
  `CLK-02` to ± 50 ppm.

🔴 **`D1`'s "10 boots without an oops" is the row most likely to be fudged, and
it is written here with the fudge named**: ten boots of *the same image* proves
less than ten boots across ten builds, and a driver that is never exercised
cannot oops. **Each driver's ten boots must include the thing the driver does** —
the timer keeping time, the GPIO toggling a pin, MTD reading 4 MiB — or the
number is a boot count and not an acceptance.

### Refutation condition, written now

> **否證 `D4`** — the two clocksources diverging in `R5-2` refutes the divisor
> chain, not the driver. That is a finding about `CLK-06` and it goes back there
> rather than being patched around in the driver.

> **否證 `D3`** — if `R5-0`'s ledger shows the register maps were already read,
> `driver-diff.md` **is not written**, and the gate says so. A diff between two
> implementations, one of which saw the other, is a diff of one implementation.
> 🔄 **2026-09-02, sharpened by the ledger rather than softened by it, and it
> now fires per driver instead of per document**: `docs/blind-write-ledger.md`
> § 4.5 records the vendor's MTD map read on the **decision** layer, so
> **`R5-5`'s L2 rows are void unless the derivation check clears them**, and
> the document says so in place rather than omitting the section. The four
> drivers with zero cited implementations — `gpio`, `wdt`, `led`, `keys` — are
> unaffected, and that split is the ledger's whole output.

> **否證 `D1`** — a driver that boots ten times and is never exercised is not
> accepted. Each acceptance names the observable the driver itself produces.

### Stop-loss, written now

* **More than 34 segments** (24 猜 + the 1.4× this board's two comparable gates
  ran at) and the remaining drivers move to `R10a` with the count recorded.
* 🔴 **If `R5-1`'s coexistence experiment cannot be built** — if 2.6.30's
  clocksource core will not read two sources — the timer step is re-scoped to a
  measurement against `CLK-02` alone and the loss of the single-variable
  experiment is recorded, **not hidden by declaring the reading good enough**.
* 🔴 **`--keep` still may not be turned on to hit a number.** `P4a`'s `L2-6`
  costs 2 bytes for a same-width link and **56** across a digit boundary; if the
  incremental build becomes the answer here, it arrives with that re-measured
  and `notes/reproducible-build.md` updated in the same commit, or it does not
  arrive.
* **If the first driver takes more than 6 segments**, the estimate is
  renegotiated in the open before the second one starts — `24 ÷ 6 = 4` per
  driver is the implied budget and nothing has tested it.

## `R4`'s step list — ✅ CLOSED 2026-09-02, in four segments

**Opened 2026-09-01** on the owner's decision, immediately after `P4b-gate`
closed. **Est. — and there are two numbers, which is the `Est.` column defect
again.** The gate board says **5**; the planning material says **3 desk + 2
bench + 3 instrument = 8**. The analysis under the gate board already settled
which one a decision should be made against: `Actual` counts segments consumed
in `LOG.md` including instrument days, and the only estimate with that
definition is the plan's. **So the budget carried here is 8, 猜, uncalibrated**,
and the board's 5 is recorded as the second number rather than reconciled.

### What this gate inherits, with its marks

| | | mark |
|---|---|---|
| `WDTCNR` is at `0xB800311C`, reset value `0xA5000000`, `WDTE[7:0]` = `0xA5` is the **stop** pattern, and `WatchDogIND` bit 20 reads `0` after a power-on reset | `SPEC.md` `REG-12` | 量 |
| `J BFC00000` **is** a reset: it writes `WDTCNR = 0` and then spins with interrupts masked, so only the watchdog can leave that loop | `SPEC.md` `LDR-33`, four sources | 讀 |
| The loader prints `Reboot Result from Watchdog Timeout!` immediately after `ramSize: 32M` where a cold boot prints a single space | `C-8`, three instances | 量 |
| `quietm` reaches a shell prompt in **7.260 s** from `J`; `loudm` in **8.98 s** | `SPEC.md` `FW-32` / `FW-27` | 量 |
| One kernel build cell takes **49 s** | `rep4`, twenty-first segment | 量 |
| The watchdog's wall-clock timeout at its two HIGH settings: **1118.133 ms** (`OVSEL=1001`) and **557.583 ms** (`OVSEL=1000`), ratio 2.0053 | `SPEC.md` `CLK-08`, `bench/2026-08-25/H3c-D4.timing` | 量 |
| The timeout at the **lowest** `OVSEL`, which is what `WDTCNR = 0` selects and therefore what `J BFC00000` fires — **2.184 ms** by halving from the measured point, never observed | derived here; `R4-1` predicts it, `R4-2` measures it | 推 |

### 🔴 Two things the plan says about this gate that this repository's own readings contradict

**① *"`F35` has already proved the loader itself writes the watchdog at
`0xB800311C`"* — 量 says it does not.** `CLK-11`: the loader **never writes
`WDTCNR`**, except two `sw zero` sites each followed by an in-place spin —
`0x804012F8` (the `reboot.......` path) and `0x804092E8`. So *the loader feeds
the dog* is false, and the premise the plan builds §1 on is not the one that
holds.

🟢 **What holds instead is better, and it may make §1 free.** `LDR-33` says
`J BFC00000` already performs exactly the recipe a scripted reset needs — write
`WDTCNR = 0`, then spin where only the watchdog can reach you. **It is an
existing loader command**, so a scripted reset may require no new register write
at all. ⚠️ **That is 讀, not 量**: nothing has issued `J BFC00000` on this
device and watched what comes back. `R4-1` predicts it, `R4-2` runs it.

**② The plan's acceptance for §1 — *"the ESC window still appears after the
reset"* — is the right test, and part of it is already answered in a direction
the plan did not anticipate.** Seating 8's reel segment 7 holds a watchdog reset
followed by the **vendor's** firmware booting whole, because after a reset the
loader re-stages `0x80500000` from flash. So *the ESC window appears* and *the
reset returns me to my image* are two different claims, and only the first is
what §1 needs. **The second is false by construction** and is the reason the
loop has to re-upload, which is what `R4-0` has to cost.

### 🔴 And one thing this gate has to decide before it can be planned: whether NFS root belongs in it at all

The plan's headline is *NFS root development loop*. **讀 2026-09-01,
`config/rlxfw-kernel.delta`: there is no `CONFIG_NFS_FS`, no `CONFIG_ROOT_NFS`
and no `CONFIG_IP_PNP` anywhere in it, and `CONFIG_CMDLINE` is
`"console=ttyS0,38400"` with `root=` deliberately removed** (Decision B — the
first boot mounts an initramfs built from this unit's own userspace).

**So NFS root is not a setting to flip; it is three config additions plus a
dependency on the vendor `rtl819x` driver coming up early enough.** ⚠️ **And it
shortens the wrong loop.** NFS root removes the image upload for a **userspace**
change. A **kernel** change still has to be built and uploaded whatever the root
filesystem is — and `R5`, the gate this one exists to make fast, is six kernel
drivers. **Whether NFS root is on this gate's critical path is a decision, and
`R4-0`'s measurement is what decides it.** Written here so that the decision is
visible if it goes the other way.

| Step | | What it produces | DoD | Where it is most likely to be wrong |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| **`R4-0`** | desk 1 | **The loop that exists, timed end to end**, on the recipe that already boots (`quietm`). Every stage separately: edit, stage + build, image assembly, get to `<RealTek>`, TFTP, `J`, first useful output. **Machine time and human time counted in different columns**, because only one of them is what `< 90 s` is about | Every stage has a number, the numbers sum to the measured total, and the sum is compared against the two numbers already in `SPEC.md` (49 s build, 7.260 s boot) rather than replacing them. **A stage with no number is recorded as unmeasured, not estimated** | **That the build dominates.** 49 s + 7.260 s is already 57 s before the upload, so the machine part may be near the ceiling with nothing removed — in which case `< 90 s` is a statement about the human stages and the DoD needs saying differently |
| **`R4-1`** | desk 1 | **The scripted-reset recipe, written and predicted before power.** What `J BFC00000` should print, what `C-8`'s discriminator should read, what an ESC window should look like afterwards, and what each of those looks like if the reset does *not* happen | A prediction block under `bench/`, frozen and committed before the seating, with `check-predictions` reporting `0 of N` at the desk. **Both directions written**: what a successful reset prints AND what a failed one prints, because a cell that can only come out one way is not a cell | **The ESC window.** If the bootcode takes a different path for a watchdog reset than for a cold one and skips the ESC window, the recipe is unusable *and that is itself a finding* — it changes `D4`'s rescue assumption, which is the sentence the whole zero-write posture leans on |
| **`R4-2`** | bench 1 | **The run**, sharing a seating rather than spending its own power cycle. `J BFC00000`, the discriminator, the ESC window, **N resets in a row**, and the **lowest-`OVSEL`** timeout — the one `WDTCNR = 0` selects, 推 2.184 ms, which no seating has observed | `Reboot Result from Watchdog Timeout!` appears where a cold boot prints a space, the `<RealTek>` prompt is reachable afterwards, and it repeats. **the 2.184 ms prediction is confirmed, refuted, or recorded as below the instrument's resolution** — and the third outcome is a real answer, not a miss: the console channel quantises at ~2 ms, so a reset this fast may be unresolvable from here, which is itself what an automated loop needs to know | **One success is not a loop.** A reset that works once and wedges on the third is worse than one that never works, because the loop will be trusted. The cell has to be N in a row with N written before the seating |
| **`R4-3`** | desk 2 | **The loop as a tool**, with its controls: edit → build → assemble → upload → reset → capture → assert, and a positive control that it can fail. The `--keep` tension is this step's to resolve or to record | The tool runs the whole loop unattended and **reports a number**; a deliberately broken input turns it red. `ci-census`-shaped arithmetic if it grows cases | **`--keep`.** An incremental build is the obvious way to cut the 49 s, and `rlxfw-kbuild.sh --keep` is marked `[TESTING ONLY]` because it breaks `.version` and therefore `P4a`'s `L2-6`. **This gate wants exactly the thing the reproducible-build gate forbade**, and pretending otherwise is how a Level-2 claim quietly dies 🟢 **DONE, in two halves.** 桌面半 2026-09-02(第二十五段):工具落地,26 個控制,斷言由建置算出。上機半 2026-09-02(第二十六段,seating 10):`--mode bench` 第一次對著板子跑完,**報出 34.74 s**,七個斷言全成立。🔴 **而 `--keep` 一個字都沒有打開** —— `R4-0` 已經量出它只買 3–5 %、而且拒絕與 `--marks` 一起跑,所以這一格的張力是靠量測消掉的不是靠讓步。🔴 **通電前一小時的稽核找到三個缺陷**,兩個安全(`S5b` burn flag 回讀、`S6b` staged head 比對,兩個都在板子上發射並通過)、一個是這個 gate 的核心(`S3` 從來沒有接到 `S2` 的樹)。控制 26 → 45 |
| **`R4-4`** | desk 1 | **The write-up**, and `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md` gains its ~~sixth~~ **seventh** entry 🔴 *(the ordinal was wrong when this row was written, and finding out why was part of the step: `P4b-gate` closed 2026-09-01 and the gate that CREATED that ledger did not put itself in it, so there were five entries where `D2`'s own property — one per closed gate — asks for six)* | The DoD read one row at a time, what the gate did not establish written, and the operating clause re-run over ~~six~~ **seven** entries 🟢 **DONE 2026-09-02(第二十六段).** `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md` 收兩筆:`P4b-gate` 的缺筆(按結案日插在 `P4a` 後面,所以 clause 讀到的順序就是它當天本來該讀到的)與 `R4` 的。clause 在**七筆六對**上重跑:仍然只指 `CPU-45`,而且是從同一對指的 —— **兩筆新條目沒有產生新的發射**。🔴 **而七筆讓 clause 自己的射程第一次看得見**:*DoD 指名了一個成品而不是它要的性質* 重複了兩次(`R3` 的 `D3` 指 `MemTotal:`、`P4b-gate` 的 `D2` 指一個 gitignore 掉的路徑),而它們**隔了兩筆**,規則寫的是「相鄰」所以看不到。**記下來,沒有去改規則** —— 因為得不到你已經想好的答案就去改規則,正是這種規則不再是儀器的方式 | ✅ |

### The DoD, split into what can be refuted

* **D1** the loop that existed before this gate has a measured end-to-end number,
  broken into stages, with machine time and human time separated.
* **D2** a reset can be triggered **without touching the power**, from a
  committed script, and the `<RealTek>` prompt is reachable afterwards — N times
  in a row, N written before the seating.
* **D3** the loop, from a source edit to an assertion about what the board
  printed, runs unattended and reports a number.
* **D4** that number is **under 90 s**, or the gate closes with the number it
  actually reaches and the reason recorded.

🔴 **`D4` is deliberately written with an escape, and the escape is the honest
half.** `< 90 s` is inherited from the plan and has never been checked against
an arithmetic this repository can already do: 49 s of build plus 7.260 s of boot
is 57 s before the upload is counted. **A DoD that can only be met by a number
nobody has verified is reachable is a DoD that invites its own fudging** — `R3`'s
`D3` named an observable that does not exist, and this is the same failure one
step earlier.

### Refutation condition, written now

> **否證 D2** — the scripted reset is refuted by the **absence** of `C-8`'s
> discriminator: if the console prints a single space where
> `Reboot Result from Watchdog Timeout!` was predicted, the reset did not
> happen and no amount of *the board came back* substitutes for it. 🔴 **And the
> reverse is a separate claim**: the discriminator appearing proves a watchdog
> reset occurred, **not** that the ESC window is reachable. Both are cells.

> **否證 D3** — a loop tool that cannot be made to fail proves nothing. Its
> positive control is a deliberately broken input — a source edit that must
> change the image, an assertion that must not match — and it has to go red.

### Stop-loss, written now

* **Two seatings** and `D2` still has no reset that repeats → the scripted reset
  is recorded 未定, the loop keeps its human stage, and `D1`/`D3`/`D4` are
  re-scoped to the machine stages alone. **This gate blocks nothing**; `R5` gets
  slower, it does not get stopped.
* **If `R4-0` shows the machine stages alone exceed 90 s**, `D4` is renegotiated
  in the open before any work is done to chase it, not after. The number to beat
  is then whatever `R4-0` measured, and the plan's 90 is recorded as an estimate
  that did not survive contact.
* 🔴 **`--keep` may not be turned on to hit a number.** If an incremental build
  is the answer, it arrives with `P4a`'s `L2-6` re-measured and
  `notes/reproducible-build.md` updated in the same commit — or it does not
  arrive. **A faster loop bought by silently dropping a reproducibility claim is
  the worst trade available here.**
* **More than 11 segments** (8 猜 + the same 1.4× this board's only two
  comparable gates ran at) and what is left moves to `R5`'s preparation.

### ✅ Read one at a time on the way out, 2026-09-02

| | verdict |
|---|---|
| **D1** the loop that existed before this gate has a measured end-to-end number, stages separated, machine and human apart | 🟢 **met, `R4-0`.** 50.4–71.1 s machine, against a served turnaround of 1149 s on the seating it read. Four of its nine predictions were refuted and they are all still in `notes/dev-loop.md` §8 |
| **D2** a reset can be triggered without touching the power, from a committed script, and the prompt is reachable afterwards — N times in a row, N written first | 🟢 **met, `R4-2`, 21/21 with N written before the seating**, and three more inside the loop tool on 2026-09-02. `C-8`'s discriminator present every time |
| **D3** the loop, from a source edit to an assertion about what the board printed, runs unattended and reports a number | 🟡 **met in two halves that have never been one command, and this row says so rather than rounding up.** The bench half ran `S4`–`S7` with **no operator gap at all** and reported **34.74 s**; the desk half ran `S2`→`S3`→`S8` and reported **39.14 s**, with `A3` linking a build made at 12:29 to a boot the board produced at 12:15. 🔴 **The seam is `--skip S2,S3`, and until that morning it was not optional** — nothing carried `S2`'s staged tree into `S3`. Fixed, and the one stage still untested in a single command is *upload the image `S3` just assembled* |
| **D4** that number is under 90 s, or the gate closes with the number it reached and the reason recorded | 🟢 **met: 73.88 s, 16.1 s of margin** — 🔴 **and it is a sum of two runs, not a measured total.** ⚠️ It is *larger* than `R4-0`'s 50.4–71.1 s, which is the honest direction: `R4-0` did not count the reset, because then the reset was a hand on a power switch. Making it a stage cost **13.21 s**, and **≈24.4 s of the 34.74 s bench half is terminator budget** rather than board |

**Refutation condition** — *`D2` is refuted by the absence of `C-8`'s
discriminator; `D3` by a loop tool that cannot be made to fail.* 🟢 **Neither
fired, and the second one was tested rather than assumed**: the suite drives the
whole runner in `--mode replay` and a wrong id, a truncated boot and a missing
recipe each turn it red (`M1`/`M2`/`M3`), while `M0` requires it green
unmutated — because a harness that kills everything and a harness that tests
nothing print the same thing. 🔴 **And the discriminator's reverse is still a
separate claim**: it appeared, and that says a watchdog reset occurred, not that
the ESC window is reachable. Both were cells and both were read.

**Stop-loss, checked** — two seatings allowed for `D2`, **one spent**
(2026-09-01) plus a ride-along on 2026-09-02. Eleven segments allowed, **four
used**. 🔴 **`--keep` was not turned on**, and `R4-0` is why: it buys 3–5 % and
refuses to run with `--marks` at all, so the trade `P4a` forbade never had to be
made. The `D4` renegotiation clause never fired.

🔴 **What this gate leaves for `R5`, and both are measured rather than
suspected**: `--iterations > 1` is now **refused** — `S4` is a loader command
and iteration 1 ends with the loader gone — so *a loop that runs once* is what
exists, and `R5` is six drivers; and **70 % of the bench half is terminator
budget**, which is the largest single block of the BENCH half — ⚠️ **not of
the loop**, because the build is 35.96 s and larger, and the build side has
already been examined by `R4-0` and `notes/incremental-build.md`. Cutting it
needs the largest-inter-byte-silence distribution measured out of the
captures this project already holds, not a guess.

### The DoD, split into what can be refuted

* **D1** a public reader, with only this repository, can find what `v0.2`
  contains without reading `plan/`.
* **D2** ✅ **met 2026-09-01, by an artefact whose PATH this row got wrong.**
  The row asked for `study/weekly-results.md` in `git ls-files`; what is in
  `git ls-files` is `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md`, five entries, one per closed gate.
  🔴 **The DoD named a file rather than a property**, and the property it
  wanted — *a public reader can read one entry per gate* — was unreachable at
  the path it named, because `study/` is gitignored and the owner ruled it stays
  that way. Recorded as a defect in the DoD's wording, the way `R3`'s `D3` was,
  rather than repaired silently.
* **D3** `CHANGELOG.md` has a section per released version, each with known
  issues.
* **D4** the take has a home named in a committed file.

### Refutation condition, written now

**This gate is not closed by four ticks.** It is closed when a reader who has
never seen `plan/` can answer *what is in v0.2* and *what has this project not
proved* from committed files alone. 🔴 **If closing it requires writing anything
into a committed file that reads like a hiring pitch, the gate has failed and
the four items go back to `P4b` at v1.0** — `plan/ARTIFACTS.md` §0's own
boundary paragraph is the standard, and it is the one rule in this project that
a release-engineering gate is most likely to break.

### Stop-loss, written now

* **More than 3 segments** and the remaining items move to `P4b`. This gate was
  pulled forward because four things had no owner, not because release
  engineering is due.
* **If `P4b-1`'s decision cannot be made in one segment**, the Release clock's
  `Contents` column is DELETED rather than reconciled — the same call the `Est.`
  column analysis already recommends, and a table with no wrong answer beats a
  table with two.
* 🔴 **No step here may be closed by editing `plan/`.** That directory is
  gitignored; a fix that lands only there has changed nothing a reader can see,
  which is the defect this gate exists for.

### ✅ Read one at a time on the way out, 2026-09-01

| | verdict |
|---|---|
| **D1** a public reader can find what `v0.2` contains without reading `plan/` | 🟢 **met.** `CHANGELOG.md`'s `v0.2` section says what it contains and no longer attributes it to a gitignored file; `README.md` § *Which gates make which version* says which gates define every version, released or not; `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md` says what it does not establish |
| **D2** one entry per closed gate, in `git ls-files` | 🟢 **met, and the row's own path was wrong** — see above. `docs/GATE-RESULTS.md`, five entries |
| **D3** `CHANGELOG.md` has a section per released version, each with known issues | 🟢 **met for the versions that exist.** `v0.0` and `v0.2` have sections; there is no `v0.1` section because there is no `v0.1` tag, and the release says it spans `v0.0` → `v0.2` rather than inventing a boundary. ⚠️ So *per released version* is satisfied, and *per version* is not — the difference is `REL-2` and it stays open |
| **D4** the take has a home named in a committed file | 🟢 **met.** `README.md`'s first screen and `CHANGELOG.md`'s `v0.2` section, each carrying the sentence that says it is a replay |

**Refutation condition** — *closed only when a reader who has never seen
`plan/` can answer what is in v0.2 and what this project has not proved, from
committed files alone.* 🟢 **Both halves have a committed owner now**, and the
second one has two: `docs/KNOWN-ISSUES.md` per release and
`docs/GATE-RESULTS.md` per gate. 🔴 **The other half of that condition —
*if closing it requires writing anything into a committed file that reads like a
hiring pitch, the gate has failed* — is the one a reader should check rather
than take.** What was written is five `did not establish` lists, a count of red
CI runs, and a table saying which of this file's own columns were copies. The
standard is `plan/ARTIFACTS.md` §0 and the gate does not get to grade itself
against it.

**Stop-loss** — three bullets, none reached. Two segments against a cap of
three. ⚠️ **The second bullet turned out not to be load-bearing**: it said
*if `P4b-1`'s decision cannot be made in one segment, delete the `Contents`
column rather than reconcile it.* The decision WAS made, and its outcome was to
delete that column anyway — and `Target` with it. A stop-loss whose fallback
is what the decision produces is not a stop-loss, it is a prediction; it is
recorded that way rather than counted as a line that held.

## `R3`'s step list — ✅ CLOSED 2026-08-31, kept as written

**Written 2026-08-28 on entering the gate, before any of it was done.** Budget
**猜, uncalibrated: 12 steps.** The gate board says 12 and `plan/` §12.1 says 13
(8 desk + 4 bench + 1 instrument). Neither is calibrated and the difference is
not worth resolving before the first step reports. **Four of the twelve are at
the bench and they are not four seatings** — `R1h-3` shares the first one, and
`probe3` runs first inside it.

🔴 **This gate is the first one whose DoD is a sentence about the device rather
than about a file, and that is why it is decomposed before it is attempted.**
*"My kernel boots to a shell and pings"* reads as one claim and is five, each
with its own observable and its own way of being faked.

### The DoD, split into what can be refuted

| | claim | the observable | what refutes it |
|:-:|---|---|---|
| **D1** | the image is delivered and entered | `---Jump to address=80500000` followed by output that is **not** the loader's | silence after the jump line — which is two causes, not one (`RUNSHEET` §B3 says so already) |
| **D2** | the decompressor runs and the kernel is entered | `Linux version 2.6.30.9 (<me>@<host>) … <date>` | the banner is the vendor's build stamp → **the vendor kernel booted, not mine** (see the anti-DoD below); or output stops inside the decompressor |
| **D3** | early bring-up completes | `MemTotal:` printed, and the console echoes | a panic before `start_kernel`, or a hang with no further output — the case the plan calls *memory-related*, and where it also says the DRAM is the loader's so the suspect is the map or the cache setup |
| **D4** | userspace is reached and the shell accepts typing | a prompt, **and a typed command returns output** | `Kernel panic - not syncing: No init found`, or a prompt that does not echo |
| **D5** | it pings | `ping -c 4` from the board gets ≥ 1 reply **and** the host's capture shows the echo request | replies with no host-side capture = something else answered; host-side requests with no replies = the driver transmits and does not receive |

🔴 **The anti-DoD, and it is written first because this project has already been
caught by it.** 2026-08-25, `R1g-4b`: *a second `J 80500000` booted the vendor
kernel*, because **the loader re-stages `0x80500000` on a watchdog reset**. So a
banner is not evidence that my kernel ran. **Every pass criterion below carries a
discriminator the vendor image cannot produce**: the `(user@host)` and date in
`Linux version`, plus one string emitted by code that exists only in my tree.
A capture without that string is recorded as *unattributed*, never as a pass.

### The two decisions this gate opens with, both made at `R3-0`

**Decision A — `R3` builds with `rsdk-1.3.6-4181`, through its own wrapper.**
It fills the half of `TC-05` that was left blank.

1. 讀 `TC-14`: it is the **only wrapper on hand that accepts `-march=4181`**, and
   `boards/rtl8196e/config.in` is `ARCH_CPU_RLX4181=y`.
2. 讀 `TC-15`: `{4180, 4181, 5181, mips1}` is the side that exposes the load delay
   slot and gets the padding; one `vmlinux` from one source through
   `rsdk-1.5.5`'s wrapper carries **21,185** load-use violations. That is a
   disqualification, not a preference.
3. 讀 `TC-17`: the drop's own top-level `.config` selects this release for this
   board.
4. 讀 `TC-16`: it is one of two toolchains that has linked a **complete
   `vmlinux`** for this board, rc=0 — and the other one is the one point 2
   disqualifies.
5. 🔴 **The objection that does NOT carry over, said plainly so the decision is
   not defended with it.** The reason to fear `rsdk-1.5.5` driving `-march=4181`
   is that its uClibc and crt are 5281-built and put three load-use violations
   into every program's entry path (`notes/rebuild-vs-shipped.md` §4). **A kernel
   links neither.** What is left against that route is that its flag set is a
   **reconstruction (推)** rather than a reading, and that is enough on its own:
   if `R3` does not boot, the vendor's own (toolchain, board, config) triple
   leaves the code generator out of the suspect list, and a reconstructed
   toolchain puts it back in.

**Decision B — the first boot mounts an initramfs built from this unit's own
userspace, not the flash rootfs.**

1. 🔴 **Safety dominates on a one-device project.** ~~An initramfs boot never
   instantiates an MTD partition map.~~ 🔴 **REFUTED on the device, 2026-08-29.**
   量, `bench/2026-08-30b/L3.log`: the `loud` boot created **two** MTD
   partitions, and the first spans `0x000000–0x130000`, which **contains both
   regions `CLAUDE.md` forbids**. Nothing was written and the decision still
   stands — on its other three legs, which were always the stronger ones — but
   **the safety margin this leg claimed was never there.** `notes/kernel-build.md`
   §16 owns the reading. *(Kept as written, because a decision's stated reason is
   worth more when it can be read against what happened.)* The flash-root route needs me to author
   one, and the two regions `CLAUDE.md` forbids — `0x000000–0x005FFF` and
   `0x006000–0x007FFF` — are inside the part a wrong map covers. 讀:
   `rtkload/Makefile` computes `FLASH_OFFSET=30000` under `CONFIG_RTL_819X`
   while this unit's kernel header is at flash `0x060000`, so **the drop's layout
   is already known not to be this unit's** and the map would be new work rather
   than reuse.
2. 讀: it is **the vendor's own supported path**, not an exotic one —
   `boards/rtl8196e/Makefile`'s header is *"Build instructions for Realtek RLXOCP
   with initramfs"* and `rtkload/Makefile` branches on `CONFIG_BLK_DEV_INITRD`.
3. **It keeps userspace as a controlled variable instead of removing it.** The
   contents are this unit's own binaries, unmodified. If the shell does not come
   up, the shell is not the new thing. Writing my own `init` would add a
   variable; the drop's `root.bin` would add three (different SDK generation,
   different model, not this unit's `H601`).
4. The plan's own stop-loss for this gate is *swap to an initramfs to halve the
   variables*. Starting there spends the halving instead of holding it, and what
   is held in reserve becomes the flash root — the **closer-to-vendor**
   direction, which is the better second experiment rather than the better first.

⚠️ **And Decision B has an arithmetic precondition that is checked before the
bench, not at it.** The image is entered at `0x80500000` and the kernel
decompresses to `0x80000000`, so the decompressed image must end below
`0x80500000` or the decompressor overwrites its own input: **5,242,880 bytes is
the ceiling.** This unit's decompressed kernel is 3,374,772 (量), leaving
**1,868,108 bytes** for everything the initramfs adds. If it does not fit, the
answer is not a smaller initramfs first — it is `LOAD_START_ADDR=0x80A00000`,
which `rtkload/Makefile` already supports for other boards and which `G0` has
already probed on this device.

| ⓐ | which toolchain rlxfw's own kernel is built with — **decided, with a refutation condition**, not preferred | `TC-05` |
| ⓑ | does the `vmlinux` this project builds actually run | `TC-d` |
| ⓒ | which `arch/rlx` `.S` files are assembled under `.set reorder`, and does any of them rely on gas inserting the load-delay `nop` | `TC-g` |
| ⓓ | the first artefact of this project that a stranger can watch working | `plan/ARTIFACTS.md` §2 |

### 🆕 The running order, which is NOT the step numbering, and why

**Written 2026-08-28 before `R3-4` was started, and it holds.** The steps are
executed **`R3-4` → `R3-5` → then back to `R3-2`'s stages 3–6**,
not in numeric order.

**The reason is that stages 3–6 build an image out of a kernel that does not
exist yet.** `R3-2` verified the pipeline's first two stages against the
vendor's own intermediates, which is what made it an instrument. Stages 3–6
— LZMA, `cvimg vmlinuxhdr`, the link at `LOAD_START_ADDR`, `nfjrom` —
consume **the** `vmlinux`, and the `vmlinux` `R3` will upload is the one `R3-4`
configures and `R3-5` fills. Running `nfjrom` first would have produced an
artefact that had to be thrown away and re-derived, and — worse — a
set of sizes and sha256s in the write-up that described a file nobody would ever
upload.

量 2026-08-28, and this is why it was not a close call: the `vmlinux` at the end of today is
**3,968,113** bytes where `R3-2` measured **3,340,287** — **627,826 bytes** of
difference, from `R3-4`'s flag (+33,248 on the file) and `R3-5`'s initramfs
(+594,578, of which 584,704 is the cpio in `.init.ramfs`). Every size and sha256 that
stages 3–6 would have produced this morning describes a file nobody will upload.

⚠️ The first version of this paragraph added `.text`'s +16,788 to the cpio's
584,704 and called the total 627,826. Those are a section delta and a file delta
and they do not add up to a file delta; the two file deltas above do.

⚠️ **What this costs**: `R3-2` stays half-done for one more step, so
`TC-d`'s image half is still open and `K4` in `notes/kernel-build.md` is
satisfied only for stages 1 and 2. That is recorded as a debt, not as a pass.

Major 90 has no chrdev, so `/dev/mtd0` opens `ENODEV` and the cell would have spent bench time on an error message. 🔴 **And `/dev/mtdblock0`, the obvious substitution, is the one node this project must not create**: `mtd0` is `0x000000`–`0x130000`, which contains the loader **and** `H601`; `mtdblock` has a write path; and `0400` is not a control, because root ignores DAC. **Declared instead: `nod /dev/mtdblock1 b:31:1 0400`** — the identical `mtdblock_readsect` → `part_read` → `rtl819x_flash` path, so the same reading, with **zero** writable nodes over either forbidden region. It cannot share a cycle with the first half, and that is mechanical rather than cautious: `0x80409A04` writes the TFTP length global so no transfer may follow an `FLR`, and after `J` there is no loader to type one into. `bench/2026-08-30c/PREDICTIONS-B5-block2.md` §13。 🟢 **收掉 2026-08-30**，電源循環 4，九格約九十秒：`Z-ab` = `00000001`（新的一次開機，儀器說的），`Z-rd0`／`Z-rd6`／`Z-rdh` 與 `V-*` 及兩份已提交基準逐位元組相同。括號的可講句子是 `G8b` 的，涵蓋 **768 / 4,194,304 = 0.0183 %** | 🔴 **`Z-rd0`/`Z-rd6`/`Z-rdh` byte-identical to `V-*` and to `bench/2026-08-24d/G8a-rd{0,6}.log`, with `Z-ab` reading `00000001`** — the last is what says this is a fresh boot at all, and without it the bracket rests on the operator having actually cycled the power, which is not an instrument. ### 🆕 Carried forward out of `R3-4`'s adversarial pass, 2026-08-28

**Five reviewers, thirty-eight findings, and the four below are the ones that
were not fixed the same day.** Everything else was: the numbers were re-derived,
the two wrong initramfs entries were removed, `kconfig-delta` gained six
controls and `mkinitramfs` seven, and `test-config-gates` gained the four
mutation classes that had none. These four are code changes to load-bearing
instruments and they get their own measurements.

| | what | why it is not fixed tonight |
|---|---|---|
| ~~**`TC-j`**~~ ✅ | 🔄 **Closed 2026-08-28/29.** `_scan_elf` is **deleted**, not left unused — a private copy of the pipeline that is still in the file is one somebody will call again. `K11`–`K16` now drive the **real CLI as a subprocess**, and a new `K17` is the control on the `HAZLINT_CHILD` guard they ride on. Not an in-process `main()`: that recurses, and the anti-recursion guard would itself be a path no user invocation takes — the same defect one level down. 🔴 **The three mutations `TC-j` named all passed 20/20 before and all fail now**: m17 (`--range` no longer refused) fails K11, m18 (`--vma-range` parsed and never clipping) fails K12, m19 (excision never runs) fails K13/K14/K16, and a fourth (m20, the guard doing more than suppressing) fails K13 — `test-hazlint.sh` **121 → 142**. **And moving them to the CLI immediately found three states `_scan_elf` accepted that the real program refuses**: a window intersecting nothing, a fixture whose every word is excised, and a `--vma-range` that missed its own site. The report now prints `unit` and `cli` as separate blocks and says **NO cli CONTROL RAN** if none did. ⚠️ `TC-j`'s claim that *CI runs zero hazlint cases* is true but is **not an oversight**: `K4`'s population control is 56 KiB of this unit's bootloader and can never be committed, so the suite exits 1 on a runner by design. 量: **all seven `cli` controls pass with `$FWRE_WORK` empty** — the part `TC-j` was about is the part that would run anywhere, and what keeps the row bench-only is `K4`/`K4b` | **Fixed 2026-08-28/29 — this column has nothing to withhold.** It was MISSING rather than empty: the row rendered with two cells against a three-column header from the day it was struck through until `spec-check`'s `C8` was generalised to every tracked `.md` on 2026-08-30 |
| **`TC-k`** | **`mips16_gaps` reads a symbol set that excludes `st_size == 0` and `STT_NOTYPE`.** 量: this kernel has **51** such symbols in its executable sections — `__copy_user`, `ret_from_irq`, `restore_all`, `resume_userspace`, `bad_stack` among them. A fixture with a violating pair between two MIPS16 functions, labelled `STT_FUNC __copy_user st_size=0`, reports **0 violations, exit 0**; the same file with `st_size=8` reports 1. The docstring's *"would have to be absent from the symbol table of a kernel that is not stripped"* is false | today's exposure is placement luck: no size-0 or NOTYPE symbol currently lands in one of the 20 gaps (largest 88 B, 406 B total). The rule needs a size bound or a second source, and picking one needs measurement |
| **`TC-l`** | **A section marked `SHF_ALLOC` without `SHF_EXECINSTR` that holds code is dropped with a green verdict.** No coverage ratio, no threshold, no non-zero exit — just a `NOT SCANNED` line. 量: a fixture whose `.text` is ALLOC-only and holds the violating pair reports 0 violations, exit 0, where 1.3's segment basis reported 1 | mitigated today and measured: on all three builds no `STT_FUNC` is defined in any ALLOC-not-EXEC section, and `.dram` (the only candidate, 26,960 B) reads 0 loads under `--raw`. The fix is a coverage floor, which is a policy decision |
| **`TC-m`** | **On an `ET_REL` `.o` the excision is printed and does not happen.** Section addresses are 0 so spans get a synthetic base while the symbol readers return section-relative values; the holes never intersect and `_cut` cuts nothing, but `print_spans` prints *"EXCISED BY NAME"* anyway. Two contradictory claims in one report, and `notes/kernel-build.md` §2.1 proposes `hazlint` over `.o` files as a build step | 🔄 **2026-08-29: `P2` is no longer waiting on it, and the reason is a measurement.** `hazlint-objs`' `Q8` reads every object whose report claims an excision — **26 of them, 27 in the marked trees — and not one scanned fewer bytes than its `SHF_EXECINSTR` sections hold**. The claimed excision removes nothing, so those bytes are SCANNED rather than skipped: the error can manufacture a violation and cannot hide one, and a 0 from `P2` is not weakened by it. **Still not fixed, still carried** — a one-line guard that needs a control, and now it also needs a decision about what an `ET_REL` scan should do with MIPS16 it cannot locate |

**And two findings that are not `hazlint`'s.** Both are closed, 2026-08-28/29,
and both turned out to be described wrongly — which is now the third session
running where that is the headline.

**✅ `CONFIG_PRINTK`.** Decided: **two declared variants from one delta file**,
`quiet` (the vendor's configuration) and `loud` (`+CONFIG_PRINTK=y
+CONFIG_PRINTK_TIME=y`), both carrying the eleven boot marks, `loud` uploaded
first. 🔴 **§6.6's trap fired on the first attempt** — setting `PRINTK`
alone takes `(NEW)` from 0 to 1 — so `PRINTK_TIME` is pinned and `(NEW)` is back
to 0. Its value is a measurement, not a default: `arch/rlx` defines **no**
`sched_clock`, so `printk_time` uses the jiffies generic and **not** the CP0
`Count` this die does not implement. Cost 量: decompressed image **3,472,384 →
3,546,112, +73,728 (+2.1 %)**, 66.2 % → 67.6 % of the ceiling, `hazlint` 0 on
both. ⚠️ **The estimate written before the build was "150–300 KB" and it was
wrong by 2–4×.**

**✅ `grep -r` over `arch/rlx`.** The blind spot is real — **13 files, 91,549
bytes, the entire BSP** — and the positive control fires (`bsp_swcore_init`
`-r`=0 `-R`=1). **Fifteen zero-claims re-run and NOT ONE is refuted.** One
enumeration moves: `TC-g`'s *seventeen* `.S` files is **eighteen**, and the
eighteenth is a linker script that changes no count.
🔴 **But the instruction itself was wrong outside `arch/rlx`, and wrong in
the dangerous direction.** At the drop root `-R` reports **79,857 paths for
66,977 real files (+19.2 %)** and follows three `romfs/tmp -> /var/tmp` links
**out of the tree**: a planted canary came back as vendor content three times,
and four of this project's own `boa` analysis logs are reachable that way today.
🔴 **And the bigger finding**: `arch/rlx/bsp` **dangles in two of the three
drops** — `target` is a tracked symlink only in `rtl819x-toolchain` — so
re-running a BSP question with `-R` across the three returns 13/0/0 and a reader
would write down *"only one drop has it"*, which is false. The BSP is in all
three at `boards/rtl8196e/bsp/`, 12 files, byte-identical but for one `#if` in
`setup.c`'s reboot path. `notes/kernel-build.md` §10, `TC-27`, `TC-28`.

| ~~**`replay-capture`**~~ ✅ **CLOSED 2026-08-31 (seventeenth)** 🆕 | 🟢 **The 60-second artefact has a better master than the footage, and it is derivable from committed evidence.** `console-capture.py` writes a `.timing` sidecar of `offset seconds` pairs — 量, `V-3.timing` has 264 of them over 849 bytes — and `scriptreplay` is on this host. Converting one to the other is arithmetic (`delay = s[i] − s[i−1]`, `count = off[i+1] − off[i]`), so the boot can be replayed into a terminal at true wire speed, byte for byte, from files anyone who clones the repository already has. **That makes the video reproducible rather than merely recorded**, and it is a better shot than either raw recording: no dead 38-second `--seconds` window, any font size | it is a new tool, and this repository's bar for a new tool is controls plus a mutation suite — `tools/test-spec-check-mutants.py` found three live mutants behind fifteen green controls **today**. | **`C10`'s exemption** 🆕 | 🔴 **One rendering defect in this repository cannot be repaired, and the reason will not go away.** `bench/2026-08-25b/PREDICTIONS-b4-block2.md` leaves a code span open, so its prose renders as code; repairing it moves the mtime `check-predictions.py` reads, and every one of its 2026-08-25 captures would then read as older than the prediction naming it. `spec-check` carries the file in `C10_EXEMPT` with its reason, and `T13` asserts each entry is still **load-bearing** — if the file is ever repaired the control goes red and the entry has to come out | the general fix is to stop using mtime as the ordering evidence — a signed or recorded first-capture time would let a frozen file be corrected. ### Refutation condition, written now

**The gate.** `R3` is not closed by a shell prompt. It is closed by a capture in
which **D1 through D5 all hold in one boot** and the discriminator string that
only my tree emits is present. Three of five is `R3a`, recorded as such.

**Decision A is refuted by** any of: `rsdk-1.3.6-4181` failing to build an object
of the `R3` configuration; `R3-1` finding that the four unexplained violations in
the 4181 `vmlinux` are **compiler output** on an executed path; or a boot failure
whose signature is a load-use hazard at a compiler-generated site. It is **not**
refuted by the shipped image being a different toolchain generation — `TC-18`
measured that channel as an instrument for code generators, and rlxfw is not
reproducing the shipped image.

**Decision B is refuted by** the initramfs failing to unpack, by `arch/rlx`
needing a source change to support it, or by the decompressed image exceeding
5,242,880 bytes with `LOAD_START_ADDR = 0x80500000`. The first two are findings
in their own right, because the vendor's own build system advertises this path;
the third has a known answer (`0x80A00000`) and is arithmetic, not an experiment.

**`R3-3` is refuted in the direction that matters by a channel that works.** If
the desk channel runs my kernel to a shell, that is **not** D1–D5 and the write-up
says so: *"runs in an emulator"* and *"runs on this silicon" are two claims, and
`R1a` has not moved.*

### Stop-loss, written now

- **Five boot attempts that do not reach D2** → stop the ladder and run the
  plan's halving experiment: the **vendor's** kernel with **my** initramfs. That
  splits *my kernel* from *my image* in one power cycle.
- **Two seatings without reaching D4** → the gate splits: D1–D3 close as `R3a`
  and D4–D5 move to their own gate, rather than a gate being held open.
- **Any capture showing a flash-write path taken** → power off, and the write-up
  starts from the capture.
- **`R3-3` costing more than one desk segment** → it stops and records *no desk
  execution channel*, with what was tried. It is an instrument, not a gate.

---

✅ **Closed 2026-08-29.** `R1h-3` ran on power cycle 1 of seating 5 and `R1h-4`

(four `cache` ops retire while `x ri` traps in the same run under the same
handler), **ⓑ is 未定 after one of the two seatings the stop-loss allows**
(`c-A` negative, and the payload's own interlock voided Group V rather than
reporting the voided cells as passes), and **ⓓ's second half has a reading**
(none of the three bits sticks, and the refutation condition did *not* fire, so
the reading carries information). No flash-write command was issued; the
flash-byte count is **unmeasured**, because no `FLR` bracket was run.
🔴 **The gate stayed open
across `R2` and the whole of `R3`'s desk work, which the re-cut of 2026-08-26
said was deliberate rather than stalled — and it was: what it was waiting for
was a power cycle it was always going to share.**

**Written 2026-08-26 on entering the gate, before any of it was done.** `R1h`
exists because `R1-gate` closed with three residuals and **an item with no owning
gate is a bug in this file's own list** — the same rule that caught `C-16` being
orphaned by `R0`. Budget **猜, uncalibrated: 4 segments** — 2 desk, 1 instrument,
1 bench, and **the bench segment is shared with `R3`'s seating rather than being
its own power cycle.**

🔴 **RE-CUT 2026-08-26, after the desk half was written.** The seating is at the
**tail of `R3`**, so `R1h`'s desk half and `R1h`'s bench half are separated by
the whole of `R2` and `R3` — and three of the five steps were written assuming
they were adjacent. **`R1h`'s desk work ends at `R1h-1`. `R1h-2`, `R1h-3` and
`R1h-4` move to `R3`'s seating preparation** and are marked ⏸ below.

**Why `R1h-2` could not stay where it was**: it produces *"the seating sheet
section, written into `RUNSHEET.md` **beside `R3`'s**"*, and `R3`'s section will
not exist for ~12 segments; its DoD (`check-predictions.py` passing on a block
written before the first capture) is a **bench-time** artefact and cannot be met
at the desk at all. A step whose DoD cannot be reached is not a step.

⚠️ **So `R1h` stays OPEN across `R2` and `R3`, and that is deliberate rather than
stalled.** Written down because the next reader of this file — including me in a
few months — will otherwise read a long-open gate as a stuck one. The gate still
owns ⓐ ⓑ ⓒ ⓓ; what moved is when its bench half is spent.

🔴 **And the ordering INSIDE that seating does not move, whatever the scheduling
does.** `probe3` runs **first**, before `R3`'s kernel. `R3`'s DoD is *my kernel
boots to a shell and pings* — and in that state the loader is gone, the DRAM is
gone, there is no `<RealTek>` prompt to type `J` into and no `DW` to recover the
result block. **"At the tail of `R3`" is when the seating happens, not the order
within it.**

**What `R1h` exists to settle**, so that closing it can be checked against
something:

Empty the build directory, rebuild, record the `sha256`, and `rb=80a02000` is the stale-build check. **In the file, not in anyone's memory** | Runs to its own end under `qemu-system-mips`; **the mutation for each cell produces the failure that cell exists to detect**; the result block is recoverable with one `DW` after the payload's own watchdog reset | 🔴 **qemu will disagree with the device again, and that is the expected case.** `probe1`'s cell 1 came back FRESH on qemu and STALE on silicon. **A qemu run that looks like the device is the run to distrust**, and every cell needs its expected-under-qemu value written down separately from its expected-on-device value |
| ✅ **`R1h-2`** | desk ½, **at `R3`'s seating prep** | 🔄 **Closed 2026-08-29 with `R3-7`, which is the same step.** `RUNSHEET` §B5's card, its **eleven** corrections (§B5-c1…c12, and `c12` is the list of what the frozen block says that is now known to be wrong), and `bench/2026-08-30b/PREDICTIONS-B5-block1.md` — twelve cells, frozen, `0 of 12` at the desk because control `N2` fires on every capture that is still in the future. ⚠️ **`R1h-3`'s own block is NOT written**, and that is deliberate: `probe3` is power cycle 1 and its cells are `R1h-3`'s to predict. *(as written on entering:)* **The seating sheet section, written into `RUNSHEET.md` beside `R3`'s** — including **two separate uploads**, because the loader re-stages `0x80500000` on a watchdog reset and one power cycle cannot run two payloads without re-uploading | `tools/check-predictions.py` passes on a block written before the first capture | **The order.** `R3` is the expensive one and `probe3` is the cheap one; if `R3` boots and takes the board away, `probe3` has to have run first — or the seating buys one thing |
| ✅ **`R1h-3`**| bench 1, **the tail of `R3`** — 🔄 **desk half done 2026-08-29** | **The run**, on `R3`'s power cycle, **and `probe3` goes first within it**. 🔄 **The desk half was not in this row when it was written and it turned out to be half a segment of its own**: `bench/2026-08-30/PREDICTIONS-B5-block0.md`, thirteen cells, frozen, `0 of 13`, **and its §0 is the card** because `RUNSHEET` §B5's card excludes power cycle 1 in its own sentence. `P7` is spent — `probe3` rebuilt 2026-08-29, sha256 byte-identical to `R1h-1`'s. **What remains is the seating**  ✅ **收掉 2026-08-29 晚上。** ⓐ 有量測（16 KiB / 16 B / 2-way，兩個控制在兩個方向都成立）、ⓒ 正向收（四條 `cache` 全 retire，而 `x ri` 在同一次 run 陷入）、ⓑ 記為未定（`c-A` 否定，Group V 被 payload 自己的連鎖 void，停損允許第二次上機）、ⓓ 後半有讀數（三個位元都沒留住，而否證條件沒觸發）。🔄 **沒有發出任何 flash 寫入命令 —— 而位元組數未量，這次上機一個 `FLR` 都沒跑。** *（這一句原本寫「零 flash 位元組」，而 2026-08-30 的掃描第一遍漏掉了它 —— 同一個檔案在同一天把同一次上機的同一句話改對了兩次，就是沒改這一列。）* DoD 的「兩個通道一致」現在是算術：`tools/rbcheck.py` 三路全部 `C93E60B5`，三個 margin 字都是 `DEADC0DE`，十個控制成立。🔴 **而這一列預言的失敗模式今天沒有發生，另一種發生了**：它寫「桌面那半的失敗模式是引用沒動過的來源、抄沒重量的控制」，今天兩者都沒犯 —— 犯的是**照著一張沒有人真的跑過的卡片**，而那是在通電前讀 `argparse` 抓到的，不是在板邊| ⓐ ⓑ ⓒ each get a reading or an explicit 未定 with the reason; **zero flash bytes**; the result block agrees on both channels — 🔄 **and that last clause is now mechanical rather than an eyeball**: the UART's `sum=`, the seal word, and a re-sum of the recovered block **minus `0x10`** (for `progress(P_SEALED)`'s re-stamp) must all agree, a procedure demonstrated on `probe2`'s committed `H2g`/`H2a` with its negative control firing before it was written down | **A fault ends the seating and takes `R3` with it.** `probe3` runs before `R3`, and every cell writes its result before the next cell starts. 🔄 **And the desk half's own failure mode showed up twice**: a value predicted from a previous payload's reading without checking whether the source file had moved since (`install.changed`), and a control quoted from a frozen block without re-measuring it (`A-catch`'s missing negative). Both were caught by the adversarial pass, neither by re-reading the draft |
| ✅ **`R1h-4`**| desk ½, **after that seating** | **The write-up.** `CPU-25`, `CPU-44`, `CPU-45` and `CPU-19` 殘留 land; `C-6`'s remaining half closes or is restated again; `R1-gate`'s decision ② gets its answer in `docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md`  ✅ **收掉 2026-08-29，與上機同一個 commit。** `CPU-25`（大小/line/關聯度，量）、`CPU-44`（retire，正向）、`CPU-45`（未定，第一次上機）、`CPU-19` 殘留（② 收、① 跟著 `CPU-45` 走）全部落地，`docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md` § ② 給出的是**下一個實驗**而不是一段論證。`tools/spec-check.py` 九個控制先成立然後全綠。🔴 **而這一列預言的陷阱正是今天踩到的東西 —— 只是踩在別人腳上**：kernel 印的 `icache: 16kB/16B, dcache: 8kB/16B` 每一個數字都是 `bspcpu.h` 的 `#define`，是「穿著量測外衣的建置常數」的教科書例子。**走訪的數字與 kernel 的數字是兩個宣稱，而這份寫上去在它們一致的時候也這樣說了** —— 同一行還帶著 `dcache: 8kB`，而那個**完全沒有量測**| `tools/spec-check.py` clean; **decision ② names a measurement or names the next experiment**, and does not name an argument | The same trap `R1g-5` walked into: a geometry number that is a build constant wearing a measurement's clothes. **The walk's number and the kernel's number are two different claims** and the write-up says so even when they agree |

### Refutation condition, written now

> **否證 ⓐ** — the eviction walk's own negative control is a victim set smaller
> than any plausible cache: **every victim must come back STALE**. If a victim
> reports FRESH at a working-set size no cache could evict from, the walk is
> measuring something that is not capacity eviction and **the size number is
> void, not approximate**.

> **否證 ⓑ** — 🔴 **NARROWED 2026-08-26: cell E is cell A's positive control in
> only ONE of its two branches, and the form below is too strong.**
> `docs/probe3-cells.md` § 6.5 and `docs/rlx-cache-and-cp0.md` § ② own the full
> statement. If E shows a **held** store, the line was resident, read-allocate is
> real, and A's *fresh* can only mean the alias is snooped — eviction being
> excluded by the far-apart pair. **If E shows write-through, it says nothing
> about residency**: ⓓ is answered in passing and A's *fresh* stays a two-way
> disjunction whose survivors no observation through the alias can separate.
> 🔴 **And the dependency runs both ways**: E assumes its own middle load
> allocated, which is what A measures — so a negative A voids E too.
> *(Kept as written:)*
> **cell E is the positive control for cell A.** If E cannot produce
> a dirty line — that is, if a store to a line the CPU has just loaded is
> immediately visible uncached — then this core is write-through after all, cell
> A's *fresh* has one fewer explanation, and **ⓓ is answered in passing.** If E
> *does* produce one, then A's *fresh* would mean the alias is snooped, and that
> is a result about the alias rather than about the cache.

> **否證 ⓒ** — a `cache` instruction that neither retires nor traps (the payload
> hangs) refutes the handler, not the instruction. `probe3` writes its cell
> result **before** issuing the instruction, exactly as `probe1` does.

### Stop-loss, written now

| If | then |
|---|---|
| **Two seatings** and cell A still cannot be made to hold | `CPU-45` is recorded **未定**, and **`R6` carries the conservative cost**: rings *and* payload buffers in the uncached window, which is what the vendor's own driver does for the rings. The throughput number in `R6`'s DoD is then measured against that, and is not compared with anything |
| `cache 0x11` traps | **That is an answer, not a failure.** `CPU-44` closes negative, the D-invalidate candidates reduce to `CCTL 0x001`/`0x200`, and **this unit's own kernel becomes a puzzle worth its own row** — it contains 37 instructions its own silicon will not execute |
| The eviction walk returns a size that is not a power of two | Record **未定** rather than the number. A non-power-of-two is the walk measuring something else, and rounding it to the nearest plausible value is how a build constant gets laundered into a measurement |
| `R3` and `probe3` cannot both fit one seating | **`probe3` goes first.** It is the cheap one, and `R3` booting a kernel is what ends a seating |
